Any other `edges` word is outside the contract (D-46). A draft preview scales `radius` (D-66). FX-LENS-001 to 018 pin it.

Lens blur's iris and highlights (D-121, proposed on 2026-09-26): parameters `iris`, exactly one of `circle`, `triangle`, `square`, `pentagon`, `hexagon`, `heptagon`, `octagon`, `nonagon`, `decagon` (n = 0, 3 to 10 blades); `roundness` in 0..100; `rotation` in -3600..3600 degrees; `aspect` in 0.1..10; `highlight_gain` in 0..100; and `highlight_threshold` in 0..100; each number held inside its range at each frame and not rounded. A file without them reads `circle`, 0, 0, 1, 0 and 100. With `k = sqrt(aspect)`, `t` the rotation in radians and `(dx, dy)` a step, `x = dx cos t + dy sin t`, `y = -dx sin t + dy cos t`, `u = x / k`, `v = y * k`, `c = sqrt(u^2 + v^2)`; for n = 0 the measure is `c`; otherwise `p = max over j in 0..n-1 of (-u sin(2 pi j / n) + v cos(2 pi j / n)) / cos(pi / n)` and the measure is `(1 - roundness / 100) p + (roundness / 100) c`. `D` is every step in the square of half-width `floor((radius + 1e-9) * max(k, 1 / k))` whose measure is at most `radius + 1e-9`, or `{(0, 0)}` when `radius < 1`. The output at `P` is `(1 / N) * sum over D of lit(input(P - (dx, dy)))`, the input read in rows top to bottom and left to right as D-116's; `lit(p)` is `p` with its colour times `1 + highlight_gain` when `highlight_gain > 0`, `alpha > 0` and `max(r, g, b) / alpha >= highlight_threshold / 100`, and `p` otherwise; when `highlight_gain > 0` each output colour is then held at or below its alpha. With `transparent` the bounds expand by `ceil(radius * max(k, 1 / k))`. FX-LENS-019 to 044 pin it.

Lens blur's blur map (D-359, 2026-10-08), after After Effects' Camera Lens Blur Blur Map group; Adobe does not publish its rule, so this is ours. `tools/lens_blur_map_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters `layer` (D-189's layer setting, "" for none), `fit` `center` or `stretch`, `channel` `luminance` or `alpha`, `focal_distance` in 0..255, keyable, and `invert` `off` or `on`; a file without them reads "", center, luminance, 0, off, and they are written only when set or already in the file, so every earlier file is the same numbers. With `layer` "" the rule is D-121's, untouched. A named layer that is not in the composition (or reads itself through a circle, refused on opening as `EFFECT_LAYER_CYCLE`) leaves the layer drawn without the effect, `EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING`. Otherwise the map M is the layer's picture, its own effects run, fitted to the drawing by `fit` (D-189); output pixel (x, y) of the drawing reads M at (x, y) held inside M, so the grown ring reads M's edge. v is the sRGB encoding of 0.2126 r + 0.7152 g + 0.0722 b of M's premultiplied pixel held within 0 and 1 (`luminance`), or its covering (`alpha`); `invert` on makes it 1 - v. With d = |v - focal_distance / 255|, J = max(1, ceil(radius)) levels, level j's iris D-121's at radius radius j / J, s = d J, j = floor(s) held at most J and t = s - j, the output is A_j + t (A_(j+1) - A_j), A_j the plain mean of D-121's lit pixels over level j's iris read with `edges` as D-121's; then, with highlight gain above 0, each colour is held at or below the covering. The layer grows as D-121's at the full radius whatever the map says. A depth pass is used by putting it on its own layer through Pass Extract (D-348) and naming that layer. A draft scales `radius` and never `focal_distance`. Drawn on the card in Lens Blur's own pass, each level's runs summed as D-121's. FX-LENS-045 to 069 pin it.

Rim light (D-117, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `color`, `#rrggbb`; `direction` in -3600..3600 degrees, `width` in 0..100 pixels, `softness` in 0..100 pixels and `intensity` in 0..100, each held inside its range at each frame and not rounded; and `blend`, exactly `normal`, `add`, `screen` or `multiply`. `tools/rim_light_reference.py` is the reference. `B` is the Gaussian blur above of the input's alpha at sigma `softness / 3`, on the grid grown by its kernel radius and zero beyond it (sigma 0: `B` is the alpha). With `u = (sin direction, -cos direction)`, exactly `(0, -1)`, `(1, 0)`, `(0, 1)` or `(-1, 0)` when the direction is a whole multiple of 90 degrees, `P` a pixel's centre and `a` its alpha: when `a = 0` the pixel is left as it is; otherwise `rim = clamp(1 - bilinear(B, P + width * u), 0, 1)`, `b = rgb / a` its straight linear colour, `C` the colour's linear value, `f = C` (normal), `b + C` (add), `1 - (1 - b)(1 - C)` (screen) or `b * C` (multiply), and the output is `((b + (intensity / 100) * rim * (f - b)) * a, a)`. No clamp is applied to `f`, so add may pass 1. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. A draft preview scales `width` and `softness` (D-66). FX-RIM-001 to 028 pin it.

Outline (D-118, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `color`, `#rrggbb`; `width` in 0..100; `softness` in 0..100; and `opacity` in 0..100, the numbers held inside their ranges at each frame and not rounded. `tools/outline_reference.py` is the reference. With `width = 0` the output is the input and the bounds do not grow. Otherwise, with `n = ceil(width)`, on the input grown by `n`, `D(P) = max I.a(P + (dx, dy))` over the whole steps with `dx^2 + dy^2 <= width^2`, `(0, 0)` included, transparent outside the input; `D` then goes through the Gaussian blur above at `sigma_px = softness / 3`, growing by `r = ceil(3 * softness / 3)` more (at `r = 0`, `D` is unchanged). With `o = opacity / 100` and `C` the colour's linear value, the output is `I + (C * D * o, D * o) * (1 - I.a)`, `I` the input, transparent outside its bounds. Its bounds expansion is `n + r`. On a drawing it runs on its own pixels before its transform (ADR-017); a draft preview scales `width` and `softness`. FX-OUTLINE-001 to 020 pin it.

Noise (D-119, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `amount` in 0..100 and `seed` in 0..100000, each held inside its range at each frame, the seed then taken as `floor(seed)`; `mode`, exactly `mono` or `color`; and `animate`, exactly `on` or `off`. `tools/noise_reference.py` is the reference. With amount 0 the output is the input. Otherwise, for a pixel with `a > 0` at layer-space pixel `(x, y)` (the drawing's own top-left pixel is `(0, 0)`; a pixel an earlier effect grew has negative coordinates), with `f` the composition frame when animate is `on` and 0 when `off`: on 64-bit words, `mix(z)` is `z += 0x9E3779B97F4A7C15`, `z = (z ^ (z >> 30)) * 0xBF58476D1CE4E5B9`, `z = (z ^ (z >> 27)) * 0x94D049BB133111EB`, `z ^ (z >> 31)`, all wrapping; `h = mix(mix(mix(mix(mix(seed) ^ x) ^ y) ^ f) ^ ch)`, a negative coordinate taken as its two's complement; `u = (h >> 11) / 2^53 * 2 - 1`. `ch` is 0 for every channel with `mono`, and 0, 1, 2 for red, green and blue with `color`. With `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))`, each channel's output is `srgb_to_linear(clamp(e_c + amount / 100 * 0.5 * u_c, 0, 1)) * a`, `a` unchanged. A pixel that does not show stays as it is. FX-NOISE-001 to 018 pin it.

Chromatic aberration (D-120, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `amount` in 0..100, a distance, and `center`, two numbers each in -1000..1000, both held inside their ranges at each frame. `tools/chromatic_aberration_reference.py` is the reference. With `w, h` the drawing's own size, `o` the growth of effects before it, the centre `c = o + (center_x / 100 * w, center_y / 100 * h)`, `R = sqrt(w^2 + h^2) / 2`, `k = amount / R`, `p` the centre of an output pixel and `d = p - c`: `sr = bilinear(input, c + d * (1 - k))` and `sb = bilinear(input, c + d * (1 + k))`, transparent outside the input, and the output is `(sr.r, input(p).g, sb.b, max(sr.a, input(p).a, sb.a))`, all premultiplied. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. For a draft, `amount` is scaled as a distance. With `amount = 0` the output is the input. FX-CHROMA-001 to 016 pin it.

Distance gradation (D-123, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `color`, `#rrggbb`, case-insensitive; `width` in 0..1000, a distance; `opacity` in 0..100; `invert`, exactly `off` or `on`; and `blend`, exactly `normal`, `multiply`, `screen` or `add`; the numbers held inside their ranges at each frame and not rounded. `tools/distance_gradation_reference.py` is the reference. A pixel is out when it is a pixel of the input with `a < 0.5` or any pixel outside the input; `d(P)` is the Euclidean distance from pixel `P`'s centre to the nearest centre of an out pixel (so an in pixel on the input's edge has `d = 1`, and an out pixel `d = 0`). With `width = 0` the output is the input. Otherwise, at a pixel with `a > 0`: `g = clamp(1 - (d - 0.5) / width, 0, 1)`, and `g = 1 - g` with `invert` on; `op = g * opacity / 100`; with `G` the colour's linear value and `b = p.rgb / a` the straight linear colour, `f = G` (normal), `b * G` (multiply), `1 - (1 - b)(1 - G)` (screen) or `b + G` (add), not clamped, and the output is `((b + op * (f - b)) * a, a)`. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. For a draft, `width` is scaled as a distance. FX-DISTGRAD-001 to 024 pin it.

Light rays (D-124, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `center`, two numbers each in -1000..1000, `length` in 0..100, `threshold` in 0..100, `intensity` in 0..10, each held inside its range at each frame and not rounded, and `color`, `#rrggbb`, case-insensitive, not keyable. `tools/light_rays_reference.py` is the reference. (1) The light `Lt` is the input pixel `p` where `p.a > 0` and `100 * max(q) >= 255 * threshold`, `q = round_half_up(srgb_encode(p.rgb / p.a) * 255)`, exactly D-89's bright test, and transparent elsewhere. (2) The rays `Ry` are `Lt` through radial blur's zoom above, `amount = length`, with its centre `c = o + (center_x / 100 * w, center_y / 100 * h)`, `w, h` the drawing's own size and `o` the growth of effects before it: `n = min(ceil(r * length / 100) + 1, 256)` with `r = |p - c|`, `p` the centre of the output pixel, and `Ry = (1 / n) * sum_k bilinear(Lt, c + s_k * (p - c))`, `s_k = 1 - length / 100 + k * (length / 100) / (n - 1)`, transparent outside the input; with `n = 1`, `Ry = bilinear(Lt, p)`, so at `length = 0`, `Ry = Lt`. (3) With `C = srgb_decode(color)` and `O` the input, the output is `O.rgb + intensity * C * Ry.rgb`, not clamped, and `min(1, O.a + intensity * Ry.a)`. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. With `intensity = 0` the output is the input. FX-RAYS-001 to 024 pin it.

Exposure flicker (D-125, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `amount` in 0..4 stops, `hold` in 1..100 frames and `seed` in 0..100000, each held inside its range at each frame, the hold and the seed then taken as `floor(hold)` and `floor(seed)`. `tools/exposure_flicker_reference.py` is the reference. With `f` the composition frame (a hidden value, as Noise's), `m = floor(f / hold)` and `u = U(seed, m, 0, 0, 3)`, Noise's hash (D-119: `h = mix(mix(mix(mix(mix(seed) ^ m) ^ 0) ^ 0) ^ 3)`, `u = (h >> 11) / 2^53 * 2 - 1`), `stops = amount * u`, and the output is Exposure's rule: linear premultiplied RGB multiplied by `2^stops`, not clamped, alpha unchanged. With amount 0 the output is the input. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-FLICKER-001 to 022 pin it.

Gradient map (D-129, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `shadow_color`, `midtone_color` and `highlight_color`, `#rrggbb`, case-insensitive; `midpoint` in 1..99 and `amount` in 0..100, both held inside their ranges at each frame. `tools/gradient_map_reference.py` is the reference. At a pixel with `a > 0`, with `b = p.rgb / a` the straight linear colour, `t = linear_to_srgb(clamp(0.2126 b.r + 0.7152 b.g + 0.0722 b.b, 0, 1))` and `m = midpoint / 100`; with the colours' 8-bit channels over 255 as `s_enc`, `m_enc` and `h_enc`: if `t <= m`, `s = t / m` and `E = s_enc + s * (m_enc - s_enc)`, else `s = (t - m) / (1 - m)` and `E = m_enc + s * (h_enc - m_enc)`, per channel; `M = srgb_to_linear(E)` and `op = amount / 100`, and the output is `((b + op * (M - b)) * a, a)`, Gradient's blend mix, normal. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. With `amount = 0` the output is the input. FX-GRADMAP-001 to 023 pin it.

Color balance (D-130, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `shadows`, `midtones` and `highlights`, three numbers each (red, green, blue), each number in -100..100, each triple keyable as one value and held inside its range at each frame. `tools/color_balance_reference.py` is the reference. With all nine numbers 0 the output is the input. Otherwise, for a pixel with `a > 0`, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))`, `L = 0.2126 e_r + 0.7152 e_g + 0.0722 e_b`, `w_s = clamp(1 - 2 L, 0, 1)`, `w_h = clamp(2 L - 1, 0, 1)` and `w_m = 1 - w_s - w_h`; with `S`, `M` and `H` the three triples, per channel `e'_c = clamp(e_c + (w_s S_c + w_m M_c + w_h H_c) / 200, 0, 1)`, and the output is `srgb_to_linear(e'_c) * a`, `a` unchanged. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-BALANCE-001 to 019 pin it.

Color balance's Preserve Luminosity (D-295, built on 2026-10-04): parameter `preserve_luminosity`, `off` or `on`; a file without it is `off`, and only `on` is written. When on, after D-130's push each pixel is moved back to its own luma by the compositing spec's SetLum and ClipColor, with D-130's weights, in display values: `d = L - luma(o)`, `o' = o + d`; with `n` and `x` the smallest and largest channel of `o'`, `s = min(n < 0 ? L/(L-n) : 1, x > 1 ? (1-L)/(x-L) : 1)` and the result is `L + (o' - L)·s`. Black and white stay black and white. A new Color Balance made in the window starts `on`, as After Effects starts it.

Offset (D-131, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameter `shift`, two numbers, x then y, each in -100000..100000, a distance, held inside its range at each frame. `tools/offset_reference.py` is the reference. With `w, h` the input's size as it reaches the effect, the pixels of effects before it included, `in(x, y)` its pixel, and `P` the centre of an output pixel: `Q = P - shift`, `X = Q.x - 0.5`, `i = floor(X)`, `f = X - i`, `Y = Q.y - 0.5`, `j = floor(Y)`, `g = Y - j`, and the output is `(1 - g) ((1 - f) in(i mod w, j mod h) + f in((i + 1) mod w, j mod h)) + g ((1 - f) in(i mod w, (j + 1) mod h) + f in((i + 1) mod w, (j + 1) mod h))`, all premultiplied, with `mod` always in `0..w - 1` (or `0..h - 1`), negative numbers included. A whole-pixel shift moves the pixels exactly. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. For a draft, `shift` is scaled as a distance. With `shift = (0, 0)`, or any whole number of widths and heights, the output is the input. FX-OFFSET-001 to 023 pin it.

Vignette (D-126, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `amount` in 0..100; `color`, `#rrggbb`, case-insensitive; `size` in 1..200; `roundness` in 0..100; `softness` in 0..100; and `center`, two numbers each in -1000..1000; the numbers held inside their ranges at each frame. `tools/vignette_reference.py` is the reference. With `w, h` the drawing's own size, `o` the growth of effects before it, the centre `c = o + (center_x / 100 * w, center_y / 100 * h)`, `m = roundness / 100`, `rx = (1 - m) * w / 2 + m * sqrt(w * h) / 2`, `ry = (1 - m) * h / 2 + m * sqrt(w * h) / 2`, and `P` the centre of a pixel with `a > 0`: `d = sqrt(((P.x - c.x) / rx)^2 + ((P.y - c.y) / ry)^2) / sqrt(2)`, `outer = size / 100` and `inner = outer * (1 - softness / 100)`; when `inner = outer`, `v = 1` where `d >= outer` and 0 elsewhere, otherwise `t = clamp((d - inner) / (outer - inner), 0, 1)` and `v = t^2 * (3 - 2 * t)`. With `G` the colour's linear value, `op = v * amount / 100` and `b = p.rgb / a` the straight linear colour, the output is `((b + op * (G - b)) * a, a)`, the blend mix, normal. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-VIGNETTE-001 to 025 pin it.

Turbulent displace (D-127, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `amount` in 0..1000 and `size` in 1..1000, both distances; `complexity` in 1..8 and `seed` in 0..100000, each held and then taken as its floor; `evolution` in -100000..100000 degrees; `speed` in -360..360 degrees a frame, all held inside their ranges at each frame; and `edges`, exactly `transparent` or `repeat`. `tools/turbulent_displace_reference.py` is the reference; the field `F` is Fractal Noise's (D-128), from `tools/fractal_noise_reference.py`. With amount 0 the output is the input and nothing grows. Otherwise, with `transparent`, the input grows by `g = ceil(amount)` transparent pixels on every side; with `repeat`, `g = 0`. For an output pixel with centre `P`, at layer-space pixel `(X, Y)` (the drawing's own top-left pixel is `(0, 0)`; a grown pixel has negative coordinates), with `f` the composition frame, the point is `((X + 0.5) / size, (Y + 0.5) / size, (evolution + speed * f) / 360)` and `D = amount (F(seed, 0, point, complexity), F(seed, 1, point, complexity))`; the output is `bilinear(input, P + D)`, transparent outside the input, with `transparent`, or D-109's held sample, the point first held inside `0.5..w - 0.5` by `0.5..h - 0.5`, with `repeat`. The bounds expansion is `g`. For a draft, `amount` and `size` are scaled as distances. FX-TURB-001 to 026 pin it.

Turbulent displace's ways (D-306, built on 2026-10-04): parameters `displacement`, `turbulent`, `horizontal` or `vertical`, and `pinning`, `none` or `all`, neither keyable. A file without one reads it at its start (`turbulent`, `none`), and only one away from its start is written. With `horizontal` the second component of `D` is 0; with `vertical` the first is. With `all`, `D` is multiplied by `k = s(u) s(w - u) s(v) s(h - v)`, with `(u, v) = P - (g, g)` the pixel centre measured from the input's top-left corner, `w` and `h` the input's width and height, and `s(d) = t^2 (3 - 2 t)` for `t = clamp(d / size, 0, 1)`. At the starts each step is the identity, so FX-TURB-001 to 026 are unchanged. B-191 pins it.

Turbulent displace's units (D-328, accepted on 2026-10-05): parameter `units`, `classic` or `after_effects`, not keyable. A file without it reads `classic`, D-127's rule exactly, and only `after_effects` is written (or `classic` where the file already said it); a Turbulent Displace added from now on takes `after_effects`. Any other word, capitals included, is `EFFECT_PARAMETER_INVALID`. In `after_effects` the amount in D-127's push is replaced by the push `a' = amount * min(size, 100) / 100`, worked in that order after both are held inside their ranges, so `D = a' (F(seed, 0, p, complexity), F(seed, 1, p, complexity))` and, with transparent edges, the layer grows by `ceil(a')`; with repeat it does not grow. Everything else (the point `p`, size, complexity, evolution, the ways and pinning of D-306) is D-127's and D-306's. At Size 100 and above `a'` is the amount, so the two units agree. **This is this program's reading, not a measurement:** no source found says how After Effects scales Amount with Size; tutorials use Amount 80 at Size 2 for a fine shimmer, which D-127 breaks into dust (P-26, tutorial 2), and the push shrinking with the wave fits every example found. For a draft (document 28), `size` is scaled as D-127 says, and the push is scaled before the amount is worked back from it, so a draft shows the full-size push at its scale. `tools/turbulent_ae_reference.py` is the reference; FX-TURB-AE-001 to 012 pin it, and FX-TURB-001 to 026 are unchanged.

Turbulent displace's new seed (D-410, Line Boil, 2026-10-10): parameter `new_seed_every` in 0..100 frames, keyable, held inside its range at each frame and taken as its floor `N`; absent 0. `tools/line_boil_reference.py` is the reference. Wherever D-127's and D-328's rules read the seed, they read `seed + floor(f / N)` instead when `N >= 1`, `seed` the held seed's floor and `f` the composition frame (P0-23's held step, rounding toward minus infinity); with `N = 0` the seed is unchanged, D-127's and D-328's rules exactly. Nothing else changes: evolution and speed are read as before. It is written only when not 0, keyed, or in the file already. Not a distance, so a draft leaves it. FX-BOIL-001 to 013 pin it.

Turbulent displace's drift (D-411, Heat Shimmer, 2026-10-10): parameters `drift_direction` in -3600..3600 degrees and `drift_speed` in 0..1000 pixels a frame, both keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; absent 0. `tools/heat_shimmer_reference.py` is the reference. With `a` the direction (0 up, 90 right, clockwise) and `v` the speed at composition frame `f`, the drift is `d = v f (sin a, -cos a)` in layer pixels, and wherever D-127's and D-328's rules read the field at the pixel centre `(X + 0.5, Y + 0.5)` they read it at `(X + 0.5 - d.x, Y + 0.5 - d.y)` instead, so the ripple slides along `d`; the push, its direction, the edges and D-410's seed are unchanged. With `v = 0` nothing moves, D-127's and D-328's rules exactly. Each is written only when not 0, keyed, or in the file already. `drift_speed` is a distance, so a draft scales it; the direction is not. FX-SHIMMER-001 to 016 pin it.

Fractal noise (D-128, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `size` in 1..1000, a distance; `complexity` in 1..20 (1..8 until D-318) and `seed` in 0..100000, each held and then taken as its floor; `contrast` in 0..1000; `brightness` in -1000..1000 (-100..100 until D-318, -200..200 until D-326); `evolution` in -100000..100000 degrees; `speed` in -360..360 degrees a frame; `opacity` in 0..100, all held inside their ranges at each frame; `dark_color` and `light_color`, `#rrggbb`; and `blend`, exactly `normal`, `multiply`, `screen` or `add`. `tools/fractal_noise_reference.py` is the reference, and holds the noise field Turbulent Displace shares. The field: `fade(t) = t^3 (t (6t - 15) + 10)`; `V(seed, ch, x, y, z)` with `i, j, k = floor(x), floor(y), floor(z)` and `sx, sy, sz` the fades of the parts past them is the sum over `(di, dj, dk)` in `{0, 1}^3` of `wx(di) wy(dj) wz(dk) U(seed, i + di, j + dj, k + dk, ch)`, `w(0) = 1 - s`, `w(1) = s`, with `U` Noise's hash `u` (D-119); `F(seed, ch, x, y, z, n)` is the sum over `o = 0..n-1` of `0.5^o V(seed, 8 o + ch, 2^o x, 2^o y, 2^o z)` over the sum of `0.5^o`. For a pixel with `a > 0` at layer-space pixel `(X, Y)` (the drawing's own top-left pixel is `(0, 0)`; a pixel an earlier effect grew has negative coordinates), with `f` the composition frame, the point is `((X + 0.5) / size, (Y + 0.5) / size, (evolution + speed * f) / 360)` and `v = clamp(0.5 + 0.5 F(seed, 0, point, complexity) contrast / 100 + brightness / 100, 0, 1)`; `G = srgb_to_linear(dark + v (light - dark))` per channel, the colours' bytes over 255; with `b = p.rgb / a`, `f = G` (normal), `b G` (multiply), `1 - (1 - b)(1 - G)` (screen) or `b + G` (add), not clamped, and the output is `((b + opacity / 100 (f - b)) a, a)`. A pixel that does not show stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. For a draft, `size` is scaled as a distance. FX-FRACTAL-001 to 038 pin it (022 and 024 superseded by D-318, 033 by D-326).

Fractal noise's look (D-299, built on 2026-10-04): parameters `fractal_type`, `basic` or `turbulent`; `noise_type`, `smooth` or `block`; `invert`, `off` or `on`; `offset`, two numbers in -100000..100000, a distance; `scale_width` and `scale_height` in 1..10000 per cent; `cycle` in 0..1000, held and taken as its floor. A file without one reads it at its start (`basic`, `smooth`, `off`, `[0, 0]`, 100, 100, 0), and only one away from its start is written. The point is `((X + 0.5 - offset_x) / (size scale_width / 100), (Y + 0.5 - offset_y) / (size scale_height / 100), z)`. Block noise sets `sx = sy = 0` in `V`, so each cell is one value on the plane. With `cycle = c > 0`, octave `o` takes each `k + dk` in `V` modulo `c 2^o` (never negative), so the picture at evolution `e` and `e + 360 c` is the same. Turbulent sums `0.5^o |V|` in place of `0.5^o V` and takes `F = 2 sum / total - 1`. Invert is `F -> -F`, before contrast and brightness. At the starts each step is the identity, so D-128's noise, its fixtures and Turbulent Displace are unchanged. For a draft, `offset` is scaled as a distance. B-184 pins it.

Light wrap (D-132, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `width` in 0..500, a distance; `intensity` in 0..400; and `blend`, exactly `screen` or `add`; the numbers held inside their ranges at each frame. `tools/light_wrap_reference.py` is the reference. It does not run in layer space (an exception to ADR-017, as an adjustment layer's effects are): steps 2 to 5 of the layer render order, the layer's other effects among them wherever Light Wrap stands in the stack, run first; then, between steps 5 and 6, each enabled Light Wrap runs in stack order in the frame's pixels. `B` is the frame drawn so far beneath the layer, as D-66 defines it for an adjustment layer (the composition's own frame inside a precomposition), and `L` is the layer after step 5, a picture the frame's size, premultiplied. With `s = width / 3`, `Bb` is `B` and `Ab` is `L.a` through the Gaussian blur above at sigma `s`, each on the frame's own size, transparent outside it, anything grown past it cut off. At a pixel with `L.a > 0`: `w = intensity / 100 * (1 - Ab) * Bb.rgb`, `l = L.rgb / L.a`, and `l' = l + w` (add, not clamped) or `1 - (1 - l) * (1 - clamp(w, 0, 1))` (screen), per channel; the pixel becomes `(l' * L.a, L.a)`. A pixel with `L.a = 0` stays as it is. Steps 6 and 7 then draw `L'` by the layer's opacity and blend mode. On an adjustment layer it changes nothing. It has no bounds expansion. For a draft, `width` is scaled as a distance. FX-WRAP-001 to 024 pin it.

Invert (D-134, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `channel`, exactly `rgb`, `red`, `green`, `blue` or `alpha`, not keyable; and `amount` in 0..100, held inside its range at each frame. `tools/invert_reference.py` is the reference. With `t = amount / 100`, at `t = 0` the output is the input. For `rgb`, `red`, `green` or `blue` (the chosen channels all three, or that one), at a pixel with `a > 0`, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))`, each chosen channel `e'_c = e_c + t (1 - 2 e_c)` and its output `srgb_to_linear(clamp(e'_c, 0, 1)) * a`; the channels not chosen and `a` are left exactly as they are, and a pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. For `alpha`, at every pixel of the buffer, `a' = a + t (1 - 2 a)`, `b = p.rgb / a` (black where `a = 0`), and the output is `(b a', a')`. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-INVERT-001 to 020 pin it.

Brightness and contrast (D-135, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `brightness` in -150..150 and `contrast` in -100..100, both keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. `tools/brightness_contrast_reference.py` is the reference. With both 0 the output is the input. Otherwise `k = 1 + contrast / 100` when `contrast <= 0`, else `k = 1 / (1 - 0.99 contrast / 100)`; for a pixel with `a > 0`, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))`, per channel `e'_c = (e_c - 0.5) k + 0.5 + brightness / 255`, and the output is `srgb_to_linear(clamp(e'_c, 0, 1)) * a`, `a` unchanged. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-BRICON-001 to 022 pin it.

Black & white (D-136, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `reds`, `yellows`, `greens`, `cyans`, `blues` and `magentas`, each in -200..300, keyable and held inside the range at each frame. `tools/black_white_reference.py` is the reference. For a pixel with `a > 0`, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))`; order its three channels from largest to smallest, ties in the order red, green, blue (a stable sort, descending): `mx`, `md`, `mn`. The primary weight `P` is the setting of `mx`'s channel (`reds`, `greens` or `blues`); the secondary weight `Q` is the setting of the pair of `mx`'s and `md`'s channels (red and green: `yellows`; green and blue: `cyans`; red and blue: `magentas`). `g = mn + (md - mn) Q / 100 + (mx - md) P / 100`, and every channel of the output is `srgb_to_linear(clamp(g, 0, 1)) * a`, `a` unchanged. A tie multiplies its weight by 0, so the order chosen at a tie never changes `g`: no cliff. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is, and a grey (`e_r = e_g = e_b`) keeps its colour whatever the settings. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-BW-001 to 022 pin it.

Posterize (D-137, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameter `levels` in 2..256, held inside its range at each frame and then floored, `n = floor(levels)`. `tools/posterize_reference.py` is the reference. At a pixel with `a > 0`, with `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))`, per channel `e'_c = min(floor(e_c n + 1e-4), n - 1) / (n - 1)`, and the output is `srgb_to_linear(e'_c) * a`, `a` unchanged. The rule is a cliff: the 1e-4 decides an 8-bit value that sits exactly on a step upward, alike in single and double precision, and no fixture channel lies within 1e-5 of a step once it is added. With `n = 256` every 8-bit colour is kept exactly. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-POSTER-001 to 018 pin it.

Threshold (D-138, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameter `level` in 0..255, keyable and held inside its range at each frame. `tools/threshold_reference.py` is the reference. For a pixel with `a > 0`, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))` and `Y(e) = 0.2126 e_r + 0.7152 e_g + 0.0722 e_b`; the output is `(a, a, a, a)`, white, when `255 Y(e) + 1e-4 >= level`, and `(0, 0, 0, a)`, black, otherwise. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The comparison is a cliff: the `1e-4` decides an 8-bit value exactly on the level as white in single and double precision alike, and the fixtures hold no decided pixel within `1e-5` of the level. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-THRESH-001 to 019 pin it.

Channel mixer (D-139, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `red`, `green` and `blue`, four numbers each (from red, from green, from blue, constant), each number in -200..200, starting at `[100, 0, 0, 0]`, `[0, 100, 0, 0]` and `[0, 0, 100, 0]`, each row keyable as one value and held inside its range at each frame; `monochrome` `"off"` or `"on"` (`"off"`), not keyable. `tools/channel_mixer_reference.py` is the reference. With the three rows at their starting values and monochrome off, the output is the input. Otherwise, for a pixel with `a > 0`, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))`; with `row_c` the row of channel `c`, or the red row for every channel when monochrome is on, `e'_c = (row_c[0] e_r + row_c[1] e_g + row_c[2] e_b + row_c[3]) / 100`, and the output is `srgb_to_linear(clamp(e'_c, 0, 1)) * a`, `a` unchanged. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-MIXER-001 to 022 pin it.

Shift channels (D-305): parameters `take_alpha`, `take_red`, `take_green` and `take_blue`, each one of `"alpha"`, `"red"`, `"green"`, `"blue"`, `"luminance"`, `"hue"`, `"lightness"`, `"saturation"`, `"full"`, `"half"` or `"off"`, starting at its own channel, none keyable. With each at its start the output is the input. Otherwise, for a pixel `p` with `a = p.a`, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))` when `a > 0`, else `e = (0, 0, 0)`; with `M` and `m` the largest and smallest of `e`, `L = (M + m) / 2`, a word reads `e_r`, `e_g`, `e_b`, `a`, `0.2126 e_r + 0.7152 e_g + 0.0722 e_b`, D-141's HSV hue of `e` over 360 (0 for a grey), `L`, `(M - m) / (1 - |2 L - 1|)` (0 when `M = m`), 1, 0.5 or 0, in that order. `a' = clamp(take_alpha, 0, 1)` and each colour channel `c` is `srgb_to_linear(clamp(take_c, 0, 1)) a'`; the output alpha is `a'`. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. B-190 pins it.

Solid composite (D-312): parameters `source_opacity` and `opacity`, each 0..100, keyable; `color`, `#rrggbb`; `blend`, `"normal"`, `"add"`, `"screen"` or `"multiply"`. Starting at 100, `#ffffff`, 100, normal. With `k = source_opacity / 100`, `o = opacity / 100` and `s = srgb_to_linear(color) o`, each premultiplied pixel `p` becomes `blend_pixel(blend, k p, (s, o))`, the layer blend of this document with the layer as the source and the solid as the destination; normal is source over. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, nothing scaled for a draft. B-195 pins it.

Channel blur (D-313): parameters `red_blurriness`, `green_blurriness`, `blue_blurriness` and `alpha_blurriness`, each 0..500, a Gaussian sigma in pixels as Blur's, keyable; `edges` and `dimensions` as Blur's (D-109, D-303). With `G_s` Blur at sigma `s` with those edges and dimensions, `A = G_alpha(p)` and, for each colour channel `c` whose sigma differs from the alpha's, `B = G_c(p)` placed so the two share their centre: the output is `A` with channel `c` replaced by `B_c / B_a * A_a` wherever `B_a > 0`, and left as `A_c` elsewhere. A colour whose sigma is the alpha's is `A_c`, so four the same are Blur bit for bit. The output grows as `G_alpha` does (none with repeated edges); a draft scales all four. B-195 pins it. FX-CHBLUR-001 to 014 pin it (D-363). D-380 (2026-10-09) adds `units` exactly as Blur's (D-321): `sigma` or `blurriness`, `sigma` when the file leaves it out, which is the rule above exactly. With `blurriness` each of the four numbers is After Effects' Blurriness `B` and its `s` is `0.3 * B`, every `G_s` taking D-321's kernel radius `ceil(6.5 * s)`, the weights normalised after that cut, and the output grows by the alpha's radius so found; four the same are Blur in Blurriness at that number. A Channel Blur added from now on writes `blurriness`. Any other word is outside the contract (D-46). FX-CHBLUR-015 to 024 pin it.

Fast box blur (D-327): parameters `radius`, 0..500 pixels, keyable, and `iterations`, 1..50, keyable and taken as its floor `n`; `edges` and `dimensions` as Blur's (D-109, D-303). With `k = floor(radius)` and `f = radius - k`, one pass is the box of weight 1 at offsets `-k..k` and weight `f` at `-(k + 1)` and `k + 1` (none when `f = 0`), over its sum `2k + 1 + 2f`; the kernel is that box convolved with itself `n` times, reaching `n ceil(radius)` pixels, laid on along each axis the dimensions name, premultiplied, as Blur's kernel. Radius 0 changes nothing. The output grows by the reach (none with repeated edges, where the edge pixel is read held for the whole kernel); a draft scales `radius`. `tools/fast_box_blur_reference.py` is the reference; FX-FASTBOX-001 to 011 pin it.

Cross blur (D-360, 2026-10-08), after CycoreFX's CC Cross Blur, whose manual does not say how the two blurs are laid together, so this rule is ours. `tools/cross_blur_reference.py` is the reference. `core.cross_blur`: parameters `radius_x` and `radius_y`, each 0..500 pixels, keyable and held inside their range at each frame, `mode`, exactly `blend`, `add`, `screen`, `multiply`, `lighten` or `darken`, and `edges` as Blur's (D-109); a file without `mode` reads blend, and it is written only when set otherwise or already in the file. `across` is the input blurred across only by one pass of Fast box blur's box (D-327, `iterations` 1) of `radius_x`, and `down` the input blurred down only by one of `radius_y`, both premultiplied; radius 0 leaves that direction unchanged. `blend` is `(across + down) / 2`; every other mode lays `across` on `down` by the general blend rule of this document, `Co = (1 - As) Cd + (1 - Ad) Cs + As Ad B(cs, cd)`, `Ao = As + Ad - As Ad`, with B the composition's mode (Add held at 1, as Solid Composite's) and lighten and darken the larger and smaller straight colour; all six are symmetric. With transparent edges the output grows by the larger of the two reaches; with repeated edges it keeps its size. A draft scales both radii. Drawn on the card as two of Blur's passes and a pass that lays them together. FX-CROSS-001 to 018 pin it.

CC Glass (D-317, After Effects' CC Glass reduced): parameters `layer` and `fit`, D-189's layer setting (the Bump Map, `""` the layer itself); `property`, as Colorama's `get_phase`; `softness`, 0..100 pixels; `height`, -100..100; `displacement`, -500..500 pixels; `light_angle`, -3600..3600; `light_color`, `#rrggbb`; `light_intensity`, 0..100. Starting at no layer, stretch, alpha, 10, 50, 100, -45, `#ffffff`, 100. The bump `H` is, at each pixel of the drawing, Colorama's phase `P(q)` of `q`, the drawing's own pixel or the map's under it (placed with the drawing's corner, clear outside it), times `q`'s alpha unless the property is alpha, blurred by this document's Gaussian at sigma `softness / 2` (none at 0), 0 beyond the blur's reach. `n = k (gx, gy)` with Bevel Alpha's central differences and `k = height / 100 * 1.25 * max(softness, 1)`. Each pixel becomes the input read bilinearly (this document's sampler, transparent outside) at its centre plus `displacement n`; then, with Bevel Alpha's `u`, `s = clamp(-(n . u), -1, 1)` and Bevel Alpha's light with `I = light_intensity / 100`. At height 0, or with no displacement and no light, the output is the input. At height 100, no displacement and a softness of at least 1 it is Bevel Alpha with `T = softness`. The layer does not grow. A draft scales `softness` and `displacement`. Reduced: After Effects' Light Height, point lights, Ambient, Diffuse, Specular, Roughness and Metal are left until asked for. B-198 pins it.

Colorama (D-316, After Effects' Colorama reduced): parameters `get_phase`, `"intensity"`, `"luminance"`, `"red"`, `"green"`, `"blue"` or `"alpha"`; `layer` and `fit`, D-189's layer setting (After Effects' Add Phase); `phase_shift`, -3600..3600 degrees; `cycle_repetitions`, 0..100; `stops`, 2..5, taken whole; `color_1` to `color_5`, `#rrggbb`; `blend_with_original`, 0..100. Starting at intensity, no layer, stretch, 0, 1, 5, `#ff0000 #ccff00 #00ff66 #0066ff #cc00ff`, 0. A pixel with alpha `a > 0` and straight linear colour `b = p / a` has phase `P(p)`: `a` for alpha; otherwise, with `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(b, 0, 1))`, `e_r`, `e_g` or `e_b`, their mean for intensity, or `linear_to_srgb(0.2126 b_r + 0.7152 b_g + 0.0722 b_b)` for luminance. With a map `M` (D-189, placed with the drawing's corner, clear outside it) the phase adds `P(M(x, y))`, times `M`'s alpha unless the source is alpha. `t = (P(p) + added) cycle_repetitions + phase_shift / 360`, and `u = (t - floor t) n` with `n` the number of stops; the colour is `C_i + (u - i)(C_(i+1) - C_i)` with `i = floor u`, the stops encoded and `C_n = C_0`, decoded to linear `g`; the output is `(g + (blend_with_original / 100)(b - g)) a` with alpha `a`. A clear pixel stays clear. No bounds expansion, nothing scaled for a draft. B-197 pins it.

Colorama's remaining controls (D-381, 2026-10-09), the rest of After Effects' Colorama on D-316's effect; Adobe publishes no formula, so the rule is ours. `tools/colorama_reference.py` is the reference. Each new parameter a file leaves out means what D-316 did, and is saved only when written or changed: `get_phase` also `"hue"`, `"lightness"`, `"saturation"` (HSL of `e`, the hue a turn as 0..1, a grey's 0), `"value"` (`max(e)`) and `"zero"` (0); `add_phase_from`, the same eleven words, the property read from the Add Phase layer (left out, `get_phase`), times the layer pixel's alpha unless it is alpha; `add_mode`, exactly `wrap`, `clamp`, `average` or `screen` (wrap): with a layer named, the pixel's phase `P` and the layer's `Q` make `P + Q`, `min(P + Q, 1)`, `(P + Q) / 2` or `P + Q - P Q`; `interpolate`, `on` or `off` (on); `opacity_1` to `opacity_5`, 0..100 (100), keyable; `modify`, exactly `all`, `hue`, `lightness`, `saturation`, `red`, `green`, `blue` or `none` (all); `modify_alpha` and `change_empty`, `off` or `on` (off); `matching_mode`, exactly `off`, `rgb`, `hue` or `chroma` (off), `matching_color` `#rrggbb` (`#ffffff`), `matching_tolerance` and `matching_softness` 0..100 (15, 0), keyable; `mask_layer`, a second D-189 layer setting, `""` none, placed by the same `fit`; `masking_mode`, exactly `luminance`, `inverse_luminance`, `alpha` or `inverse_alpha` (luminance); `composite_over`, `on` or `off` (on). A pixel is worked when `a > 0`, or always when both Modify Alpha and Change Empty Pixels are on, an empty pixel read as black with phase from black. The place `u`, `i` and `f = u - i` as D-316's, `f = 0` with interpolate off; the ring colour `m = C_i + f (C_(i+1) - C_i)` encoded, and the opacity `o_i + f (o_(i+1) - o_i)` alike. The modified colour `g`: `m` for all; `e` with the hue, lightness or saturation of `m` (HSL) for those three; `e` with that one channel of `m` for red, green or blue; `e` for none; decoded to linear and held inside 0..1. The new alpha `a'` is the ring's opacity with Modify Alpha on, else `a`; `R = (g a', a')`. The weight `w` is 1, times Change Color's nearness (D-370: `1` within the tolerance, falling straight to `0` across the softness, the distance by `rgb`, `hue` or `chroma` as Change Color's, of `e` from the matching colour) when matching is on, times the mask pixel's encoded luminance times its alpha, its alpha, or one less either, when a mask layer is named. With `O` the pixel as it was, premultiplied: composite on `X = O + w (R - O)`, off `X = w R`; the output is `X + (blend_with_original / 100)(O - X)`, all four channels. With every new parameter at its start this is D-316's sum exactly, worked by the same arithmetic, so an old file draws to the bit what it drew. A mask layer not in the composition is no mask, with `EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING` on opening and each frame (D-189). An Add Phase or mask layer keeps the effect on the processor; the rest is on the card in the colour pass. B-260 pins it.

Gamma/Pedestal/Gain (D-382, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' Gamma/Pedestal/Gain, whose method Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/gamma_pedestal_gain_reference.py` is the reference. `core.gamma_pedestal_gain`: parameters `black_stretch` in 1..4 (1); for each of red, green and blue `red_gamma`, `green_gamma`, `blue_gamma` in 0.1..10 (1), `red_pedestal`, `green_pedestal`, `blue_pedestal` in -1..1 (0) and `red_gain`, `green_gain`, `blue_gain` in 0..4 (1); all ten keyable, held inside their range and written. At a pixel with covering a > 0, e its straight colour through the sRGB curve held inside 0..1 and s the black stretch, each channel first `x = s e / (1 + (s - 1) e)`, which keeps 0 and 1 and lifts the dark values more the larger s is; then `pedestal + (gain - pedestal) x^(1 / gamma)` with that channel's three, a gamma above 1 lightening the middle as Levels' does and a pedestal above the gain turning the channel over; the result held inside 0..1 (the effect works on 8-bit values, as After Effects' does), back to linear at covering a. The covering is never changed; a pixel with covering 0 stays as it is; with every setting at its start the layer is left exactly as it is. **Deviations:** the stretch curve and the direction of gamma are ours. Drawn on the card in the colour pass (mode 31, the power in single precision as Levels'), fused in a colour run. FX-GPG-001 to 021 pin it; B-261 pins the build.

Vibrance (D-140, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `vibrance` and `saturation`, each in -100..100, each keyable and held inside its range at each frame. `tools/vibrance_reference.py` is the reference. With both 0 the output is the input. Otherwise, for a pixel with `a > 0`, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))`, `L = 0.2126 e_r + 0.7152 e_g + 0.0722 e_b`, `s = max(e) - min(e)` and `k = 1 + saturation / 100 + vibrance / 100 (1 - s)`; per channel `e'_c = L + (e_c - L) k`, and the output is `srgb_to_linear(clamp(e'_c, 0, 1)) * a`, `a` unchanged. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-VIBRANCE-001 to 018 pin it.

Broadcast safe (D-365, 2026-10-08), after After Effects' Broadcast Colors, whose method Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/broadcast_safe_reference.py` is the reference. `core.broadcast_safe`: parameters `locale`, exactly `ntsc` or `pal`; `method`, exactly `reduce_luminance`, `reduce_saturation`, `key_out_unsafe` or `key_out_safe`; and `max_amplitude` M in 90..120 IRE, keyable and held inside its range at each frame; all three are written. At a pixel with covering a > 0, e its straight colour through the sRGB curve held inside 0..1: `Y = 0.299 eR + 0.587 eG + 0.114 eB`, `C = sqrt((0.492 (eB - Y))^2 + (0.877 (eR - Y))^2)`, and the signal's top `amp = s + (100 - s)(Y + C)` with the set-up s 7.5 for ntsc and 0 for pal. The pixel is unsafe when `amp > M + 1e-4` (the 1e-4 IRE so a value on the limit, white at 100 among them, is not tipped over by single precision). `key_out_unsafe` clears an unsafe pixel to all four 0 and leaves every other exactly; `key_out_safe` the other way round. Otherwise, with `m = (M - s) / (100 - s)`, a safe pixel is e, an unsafe one is `e m / (Y + C)` for `reduce_luminance`, and for `reduce_saturation` `Y + (e - Y)(m - Y) / C` when `Y < m` and C > 0, else the grey (m, m, m); the result, held inside 0..1, back to linear at covering a (the shared colour rule). **Space (a deviation from linear light):** a video signal is made from gamma-encoded R'G'B', so the signal is measured on the sRGB-encoded colour as if it were video's, not on linear values nor on Rec. 601 or 709 video levels; only the top of the signal is checked, not a dip below black; float values above 1 are held to 1 first. Drawn on the card in the colour pass, fused in a colour run. FX-BCAST-001 to 015 pin it.

Color neutralizer (D-366, 2026-10-08), after CycoreFX's CC Color Neutralizer, whose manual says what each control does and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/color_neutralizer_reference.py` is the reference. `core.color_neutralizer`: parameters `shadows_unbalance`, `midtones_unbalance` and `highlights_unbalance`, each `#rrggbb` (#000000, #808080, #ffffff); `shadows`, `midtones` and `highlights`, each three numbers red, green, blue in -255..255 levels (0, 0, 0); `pinning` in 0..100 (0); `black_point` and `white_point` in 0..255 (0 and 255); every number keyable and held inside its range, all written. For each reference r, u its unbalance colour over 255 and t_r its numbers, the correction is `d_r = (luma(u) - u) + t_r / 255` channel by channel, `luma` the 709 weights 0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722. At a pixel with covering a > 0, e as Broadcast safe's and `v = 255 luma(e)`: `t = clamp((v - black) / (white - black), 0, 1)` when white > black, else 1 when `v + 1e-4 >= black` and 0 below (the 1e-4 level as D-138's). The correction is `d_s + (d_m - d_s) 2t` for t <= 1/2 and `d_m + (d_h - d_m)(2t - 1)` above; its weight is 1 when pinning is 0, else `clamp(min(t, 1 - t) / (pinning / 200), 0, 1)`; the output is e plus weight times correction, held inside 0..1, back to linear at covering a. When all three corrections are 0 the layer is left exactly as it is. **Deviations:** worked on the sRGB-encoded colour, as every colour effect here, where CycoreFX works on stored values; the Unbalance colours and the numbers add, where CycoreFX's picking a colour sets the numbers; no averaging eyedropper (pick the colour in the colour box); no View Correction graph; Blend w. Original is the Mix every effect has. Drawn on the card in the colour pass, fused in a colour run. FX-NEUTRAL-001 to 018 pin it.

Color offset (D-367, 2026-10-08), after CycoreFX's CC Color Offset, whose manual names the overflows without formulas, so this rule is ours. `tools/color_offset_reference.py` is the reference. `core.color_offset`: parameters `red_phase`, `green_phase` and `blue_phase` in -3600..3600 degrees (0), keyable and held inside their range, and `overflow`, exactly `wrap`, `solarize` or `polarize` (wrap); all written. At a pixel with covering a > 0, e as Broadcast safe's, each channel v with phase P and `f = P / 360` turns: a channel whose phase is 0 is v; `wrap` is `u = v + f`, u itself when 0 <= u <= 1, else `u - floor(u)` (a u within 1e-9 of a whole number taken as it, so rounding cannot send a value round the other way); `solarize` is `1 - |rem_euclid(u, 2) - 1|`; `polarize` is `(1 - cos(acos(1 - 2v) + pi f)) / 2`, worked as `(1 - (1 - 2v) cos(pi f) + 2 sqrt(v (1 - v)) sin(pi f)) / 2`. The result, held inside 0..1, back to linear at covering a; with all three phases 0 the layer is left exactly as it is. **Deviations:** worked on the sRGB-encoded colour where CycoreFX turns the stored 0..255 values; with wrap a whole turn leaves every value between as it was but turns black to white and white to black (0 + 1 = 1 is kept, 1 + 1 = 2 goes round to 0), as black and white are one point on the circle; solarize and polarize fold to the negative at one turn and back at two. Drawn on the card in the colour pass, fused in a colour run. FX-COFFSET-001 to 016 pin it.

Kernel (D-368, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Kernel, whose manual says what each control does and gives no formula, so this rule is ours. `tools/kernel_reference.py` is the reference. `core.kernel`: parameters `line_1`, `line_2` and `line_3`, three numbers each in -1000..1000, the grid's top, middle and bottom rows ("0, 0, 0", "0, 1, 0", "0, 0, 0": the middle pixel alone); `divider` in 0.01..1000 (1); every number keyable and held inside its range; `absolute_values`, exactly `off` or `on` (off); all written. A pixel's value v is its straight colour through the sRGB curve held inside 0..1, channel by channel, or 0 for a pixel with covering 0; past the layer's border the border pixel repeats, as Find Edges' neighbours do. At a pixel (x, y) with covering a > 0, each channel's sum is `S = sum over j, i in 0..2 of line_(j+1)[i] v(x + i - 1, y + j - 1)`: line 1 weighs the row above and each line's first number the pixel to the left. Then `u = S / divider`, `|u|` when `absolute_values` is on, held inside 0..1, back to linear at covering a; the covering is kept, a pixel with covering 0 stays as it is, and the layer does not grow. When the grid over the divider is the middle pixel alone (the middle number over the divider exactly 1, every other number 0) the layer is left exactly as it is. **Deviations:** worked on the sRGB-encoded colour, where CycoreFX works on stored values; the result is held inside 0..1, so a negative sum is black, as CycoreFX's is at 8 and 16 bits; CC Kernel's Blend w. Original is the Mix every effect has (D-202); no Offset or Alpha control, as the manual (HD 1.8.9) has none. Drawn on the card in its own pass (the neighbour pass of Emboss and Find Edges). FX-KERNEL-001 to 018 pin it.

Toner (D-369, 2026-10-09), after CycoreFX's CC Toner, whose manual gives no formula, so this rule is ours; it shares Gradient map's lightness (D-129) and is its own effect. `tools/toner_reference.py` is the reference. `core.toner`: parameters `tones`, exactly `duotone`, `tritone` or `pentone` (tritone); `highlights`, `brights`, `midtones`, `darktones` and `shadows`, each `#rrggbb` (#ffffff, #e0cfb0, #8c7355, #46382a, #000000, a warm sepia of our own, CycoreFX's starting colours not being published); all written. The ramp's stops, each colour's 8-bit channels over 255: duotone shadows and highlights; tritone shadows, midtones and highlights; pentone shadows, darktones, midtones, brights and highlights; evenly spaced from 0 to 1, a tone the choice leaves out kept but not used. At a pixel with covering a > 0, with b its straight linear colour, `t = linear_to_srgb(clamp(0.2126 b.r + 0.7152 b.g + 0.0722 b.b, 0, 1)) (n - 1)` for n stops, `k = min(floor(t), n - 2)`, and the output is `stop_k + (t - k)(stop_(k+1) - stop_k)` channel by channel, held inside 0..1, back to linear at covering a. A pixel with covering 0 stays as it is. **Deviations:** CC Toner's Blend w. Original is the Mix every effect has (D-202); no eyedroppers (pick the colours in the colour boxes). Drawn on the card in the colour pass, fused in a colour run. FX-TONER-001 to 013 pin it.

Change color (D-370, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' Change Color, whose method Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours; it shares Change to Color's HLS (D-197) and is its own effect. `tools/change_color_reference.py` is the reference. `core.change_color`: parameters `view`, exactly `corrected` or `mask` (corrected); `hue_transform` in -3600..3600 degrees (0); `lightness_transform` and `saturation_transform` in -100..100 (0); `color_to_change`, `#rrggbb` (#ff0000); `tolerance` and `softness` in 0..100 (15 and 0); every number keyable and held inside its range; `match_colors`, exactly `rgb`, `hue` or `chroma` (hue); `invert_mask`, exactly `off` or `on` (off); all written. At a pixel with covering a > 0, e as Broadcast safe's and c the colour to change, its 8-bit channels over 255, the distance d is: for `rgb`, `|e - c| / sqrt(3)`; for `hue`, the angle between their HLS hues over 180, or 1 when either is a grey; for `chroma`, the distance between their `(Cb, Cr) = ((B - Y) / 1.8556, (R - Y) / 1.5748)` with `Y = 0.2126 R + 0.7152 G + 0.0722 B`. With `t = tolerance / 100` and `s = softness / 100` the mask k is 1 when `d <= t`, 0 when s is 0 or `d >= t + s`, else `1 - (d - t) / s`; `1 - k` when `invert_mask` is on. With `view` `mask` the pixel is the grey k, back to linear at covering a. Otherwise a pixel with k = 0 is its input exactly; else, (h, l, s) its HLS (h 0 for a grey), `h' = h + hue_transform` taken into [0, 360), and with `p = lightness_transform / 100`, `l' = l + (1 - l) p` when p >= 0, else `l (1 + p)`, and s' from s the same way with `saturation_transform`; the colour of (h', l', s') as Change to Color's, and the output `e + k (rgb - e)`, held inside 0..1, back to linear at covering a. When all three transforms are 0 and the view is `corrected` the layer is left exactly as it is. **Deviations:** worked on the sRGB-encoded colour; a grey has no hue, so under hue matching it is never near a colour; the chroma distance is the plain HD colour-difference distance, not scaled to 0..1, and softness is an absolute distance beyond the tolerance, not a share of it as in Change to Color; no eyedropper (pick the colour in the colour box). Drawn on the card in the colour pass, fused in a colour run. FX-CHCOLOR-001 to 024 pin it.

Color balance (HLS) (D-374, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' Color Balance (HLS), whose method Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours; it shares Change to Color's HLS (D-197) and is its own effect, apart from Hue/Saturation (D-113), whose lightness pushes toward black or white by a share of what is left where this one adds its amounts straight on. `tools/color_balance_hls_reference.py` is the reference. `core.color_balance_hls`: parameters `hue` in -3600..3600 degrees (0); `lightness` and `saturation` in -100..100 (0); all three keyable and held inside their range; all written. At a pixel with covering a > 0, e its straight colour through the sRGB curve held inside 0..1 and (h, l, s) its HLS (`l = (max + min) / 2`, `s = (max - min) / (1 - |2l - 1|)`; a grey, max = min, has no hue and s = 0): `h' = h + hue` taken into [0, 360), `l' = clamp(l + lightness / 100, 0, 1)`, `s' = clamp(s + saturation / 100, 0, 1)`, a grey keeping s' = 0 whatever the saturation, having no hue to show. The colour of (h', l', s'), held inside 0..1, back to linear at covering a; a pixel with covering 0 stays as it is; with all three 0 the layer is left exactly as it is. **Deviations:** worked on the sRGB-encoded colour. Drawn on the card in the colour pass, fused in a colour run. FX-HLSBAL-001 to 014 pin it.

Color link (D-375, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' Color Link, whose method Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours; it reads D-351's statistics (Stretch Levels') and lays the colour on with Paraffin's six mixers (D-185). `tools/color_link_reference.py` is the reference. `core.color_link`: parameters `layer`, a layer of this composition by id, "" (the layer itself as the effects before this one left it); `sample`, exactly `average`, `median`, `brightest`, `darkest`, `max_rgb` or `min_rgb` (average); `clip` in 0..49 per cent (5); `stencil`, exactly `off` or `on` (off); `opacity` in 0..100 (100); `blending_mode`, exactly `normal`, `multiply`, `screen`, `add`, `overlay` or `soft_light` (normal); clip and opacity keyable and held inside their range; all written. The picture read: with a layer named, that layer's own picture as D-189's layer setting makes it (its drawing, masks and effects, steps 1 to 3), but whole, not fitted to the holder (the fit word `whole`, which no setting writes), so a bigger layer is read to its edges; with "" the holder's picture as the effects before this one left it. Its statistics are D-351's: the four 256-bin histograms of the encoded straight colour (red, green, blue and brightness, each pixel adding its covering) and, for each brightness bin, the colour it holds. A picture where nothing shows has no statistics, and the layer is left as it is; a name that is not a layer of this composition is `EFFECT_LAYER_MISSING`, on opening and at each frame (D-189), and the layer is left as it is. The colour C, each channel 0..1, kb and kw D-351's clip points at `clip` each end: `average`, per channel the mean bin over 255 of bins kb to kw; `median`, per channel the lowest bin whose running total is above half, over 255; `max_rgb` and `min_rgb`, per channel kw and kb over 255; `brightest` and `darkest`, the colour held by brightness bin kw and kb. With f = mix(e, C) per channel by the blending mode, o = opacity / 100, e the pixel's straight colour through the sRGB curve held inside 0..1 and a its covering: stencil `on`, at a > 0, `e + o (f - e)` at covering a; stencil `off`, one layer of colour C at opacity o laid over the pixel, the empty pixels too: `a' = o + a (1 - o)`, colour `(o (1 - a) C + o a f + (1 - o) a e) / a'`. Either result held inside 0..1, back to linear at its covering; opacity 0 leaves the layer exactly as it is. **Deviations:** the named layer is read whole, with its masks and effects; no alpha samples (After Effects' Average Alpha and the like); six blending modes, not After Effects' full list; the 0..49 clip range is ours; Stencil is After Effects' Stencil Original Alpha. Drawn on the card in the colour pass, fused in a colour run, when a layer is named; the layer's own picture stays on the processor, the card not reading a layer's colour back yet. FX-CLINK-001 to 025 pin it.

Color stabilizer (D-376, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' Color Stabilizer, whose method Adobe does not publish, so this rule is ours. `tools/color_stabilizer_reference.py` is the reference. `core.color_stabilizer`: parameters `stabilize`, exactly `brightness`, `levels` or `curves` (brightness); `reference_frame`, the composition frame whose colours are kept, 0..1000000, taken down to a whole number (0), standing in for After Effects' Set Frame button; `black_point`, `mid_point` and `white_point`, points in per cent of the layer's width and height, -1000..1000 ((25, 50), (50, 50), (75, 50)); `sample_size`, each sample's radius in pixels, 0..100 (5), a distance and so scaled for a draft; the points and sample size keyable and held inside their range; all written. A point (u, v) per cent is at (u W / 100, v H / 100) in the layer's W by H picture. Its sample is the mean encoded straight colour, each pixel weighted by its covering, of the pixels whose centres (x + 1/2, y + 1/2) lie within the radius of it; when none does, the one pixel holding the point (its column and row taken down, held inside the picture); a sample with no covering is none. The reference samples are taken from the layer's picture at the reference frame with the effects before this one, at the points and size the settings have at that frame; the current samples from this frame's picture, at this frame's settings. A missing sample, or a reference frame outside the layer, leaves the frame as it is. `brightness`: with `Y = 0.2126 R + 0.7152 G + 0.0722 B`, every channel moves by Y(reference black) - Y(current black). `levels`: each channel mapped by the straight line through the pairs (current, reference) of the black and white samples; `curves`, through the three pairs of black, mid and white, a line joining each pair to the next in order of the current value and the outer lines carried on past the ends. A pair whose current value is within a millionth of the one before is left out; with one pair left the channel moves by reference less current. Each result held inside 0..1, back to linear at the pixel's covering; a pixel with covering 0 stays as it is. On an adjustment layer the frames beneath at other times are not to hand: there is no reference, the frame is left as it is, with `TEMPORAL_SMOOTHING_SKIPPED` each frame (document 28). **Deviations:** a number for the reference frame where After Effects has a Set Frame button; Curves joins the samples with straight lines, Adobe's curve shape not being published; the samples are encoded colours, not stored values. Drawn on the processor, since the reference frame is read there; the card draws the rest of the frame round it. FX-CSTAB-001 to 016 pin it.

Leave color (D-141, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `color`, `#rrggbb`, case-insensitive; `tolerance`, `softness` and `amount` in 0..100, all three held inside their range at each frame. `tools/leave_color_reference.py` is the reference. The hue of an encoded colour, in degrees 0..360, is the HSV hue: with `mx`, `mn` the largest and smallest channel, none when `mx == mn`; else when red is the largest (checked first), `60 * ((g - b) / (mx - mn))` taken into [0, 360); when green, `60 * ((b - r) / (mx - mn) + 2)`; when blue, `60 * ((r - g) / (mx - mn) + 4)`. `h_c` is the hue of the colour's 8-bit channels over 255. At a pixel with `a > 0`, with `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))` the encoded straight colour and `h` its hue: `dist = min(|h - h_c|, 360 - |h - h_c|) / 180`, or `dist = 1` when either hue is none; `t = tolerance / 100`, `s = softness / 100`; `k = 1` when `dist <= t`, else `k = 0` when `s == 0` or `dist >= t + s`, else `k = 1 - (dist - t) / s`; `d = amount / 100 * (1 - k)`, `Y = 0.2126 e_r + 0.7152 e_g + 0.0722 e_b`, `e'_c = e_c + d * (Y - e_c)`, and the output is `(srgb_to_linear(clamp(e', 0, 1)) * a, a)`. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. At softness 0 the tolerance is a cliff; the fixtures keep every pixel's `dist` more than 1e-5 from it. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. With `amount = 0` the output is the input. FX-LEAVE-001 to 022 pin it.

Solarize (D-142, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameter `threshold` in 0..255, keyable and held inside its range at each frame. `tools/solarize_reference.py` is the reference. For a pixel with `a > 0`, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))`; per channel `e'_c = 1 - e_c` when `255 e_c + 1e-4 >= threshold`, else `e_c`, and the output is `srgb_to_linear(e'_c) * a`, `a` unchanged; a channel that is kept is the input's own value. The choice is a cliff: the added 1e-4 decides an 8-bit value on the threshold as inverted, and no fixture channel sits within 1e-5 of the cliff. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-SOLAR-001 to 016 pin it.

Halftone (D-143, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `size` in 2..200, a distance; `angle` in degrees, -3600..3600; `ink` and `paper`, each `#rrggbb`, case-insensitive; and `amount` in 0..100; the numbers held inside their ranges at each frame. `tools/halftone_reference.py` is the reference. At a pixel with `a > 0`, with `(X, Y) = (x - o_x + 0.5, y - o_y + 0.5)` its centre in drawing coordinates (`o` the growth of effects before it) and `θ = angle * pi / 180`: `u = (X cos θ + Y sin θ) / size`, `v = (-X sin θ + Y cos θ) / size` and `ρ = sqrt((u - floor(u) - 0.5)^2 + (v - floor(v) - 0.5)^2)`. With `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))` the encoded straight colour, the darkness `k = 1 - clamp(0.2126 e_r + 0.7152 e_g + 0.0722 e_b, 0, 1)` and the dot's radius `r = sqrt(k / pi)` when `k <= pi / 4`, else `r = 0.5 + 0.21 * (k - pi / 4) / (1 - pi / 4)`. The pixel is ink when `ρ < r`, a cliff. With `G` the ink's linear value if ink, else the paper's, `op = amount / 100` and `b = p.rgb / a` the straight linear colour, the output is `((b + op * (G - b)) * a, a)`, the blend mix, normal; at `op = 0` the pixel stays exactly as it is. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. A draft scales `size`. FX-HALFTONE-001 to 025 pin it, with every decided pixel at least 1e-5 from its cliff.

Mosaic (D-144, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameter `size` in 1..1000, a distance, held inside its range at each frame. `tools/mosaic_reference.py` is the reference. On the input buffer, `w` by `h` pixels including any growth of effects before it, with `o` the drawing's own corner in that buffer: pixel `(x, y)` is in block `(floor((x - o_x) / size), floor((y - o_y) / size))`. Every pixel of a block becomes the mean of all the buffer's pixels in that block, all four premultiplied channels alike, the pixels with `a = 0` counted; a block that runs past the buffer's edge takes only the pixels inside it, so a partly covered block is partly transparent. A `size` below 1 after a draft's scaling makes one-pixel blocks, and the output is the input; size 1 likewise. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. A draft scales `size`. FX-MOSAIC-001 to 020 pin it, with every pixel either exactly on a block's edge, at a size single precision holds exactly, or at least 1e-5 from one.

Emboss (D-145, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `direction` in -3600..3600 degrees, `relief` in 0..100, a distance, and `contrast` in 0..1000, each keyable and held inside its range at each frame, and `mode`, "grey" or "color", not keyable. `tools/emboss_reference.py` is the reference. `u = (sin direction, -cos direction)`, exactly `(0, -1)`, `(1, 0)`, `(0, 1)` or `(-1, 0)` when the direction is a whole multiple of 90 degrees, and `d = relief u`. The picture luma of a premultiplied pixel is `y(p) = linear_to_srgb(clamp(0.2126 p.r + 0.7152 p.g + 0.0722 p.b, 0, 1))`. For a pixel with `a > 0` and centre `P`, with `s(q)` the bilinear sample of the input at `q`, the point first held inside the input's pixel centres (D-109's held sample): `v = 0.5 + (y(s(P + d)) - y(s(P - d))) contrast / 100`. With `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))`: grey, `e'_c = v` for every channel; color, `e'_c = e_c + v - 0.5`; the output is `srgb_to_linear(clamp(e'_c, 0, 1)) * a`, `a` unchanged. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. Relief 0 or contrast 0 gives `v = 0.5`. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. For a draft, `relief` is scaled as a distance. FX-EMBOSS-001 to 026 pin it.

Find Edges (D-146, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `invert`, exactly `off` or `on`, not keyable; and `amount` in 0..100, held inside its range at each frame. `tools/find_edges_reference.py` is the reference. At `amount = 0` the output is the input. With `y(p) = linear_to_srgb(clamp(0.2126 p.r + 0.7152 p.g + 0.0722 p.b, 0, 1))` the picture luma of a premultiplied pixel (its colour over black, so an `a = 0` pixel has luma 0) and `Yp(x, y) = y(input pixel (clamp(x, 0, w - 1), clamp(y, 0, h - 1)))`, `w, h` the buffer's size: `gx = Yp(x+1, y-1) + 2 Yp(x+1, y) + Yp(x+1, y+1) - Yp(x-1, y-1) - 2 Yp(x-1, y) - Yp(x-1, y+1)`, `gy = Yp(x-1, y+1) + 2 Yp(x, y+1) + Yp(x+1, y+1) - Yp(x-1, y-1) - 2 Yp(x, y-1) - Yp(x+1, y-1)`, `m = min(1, sqrt(gx^2 + gy^2) / 2)`, and `v = 1 - m` for `off` or `v = m` for `on`. At a pixel with `a > 0`, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))`, `e'_c = e_c + amount / 100 (v - e_c)` for each channel, and the output is `(srgb_to_linear(clamp(e'_c, 0, 1)) * a, a)`; a pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-FINDEDGES-001 to 019 pin it.

Sharpen (D-147, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `amount` in 0..500 (100) and `radius` in 0..100 (1, a distance, scaled for a draft), each keyable and held inside its range at each frame. `tools/sharpen_reference.py` is the reference. With amount 0 or radius 0 the output is the input. Otherwise `B` is the input through the Gaussian blur above at sigma `radius`, transparent outside the layer, read at the input's own pixels (its growth cut off). For a pixel with `a > 0`, `e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1))` and `eb = linear_to_srgb(clamp(B.rgb / B.a, 0, 1))` when `B.a > 0`, else `eb = e`; per channel `e'_c = e_c + amount / 100 (e_c - eb_c)`, and the output is `srgb_to_linear(clamp(e'_c, 0, 1)) * a`, `a` unchanged. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. Dividing the blur by its own covering makes a flat colour beside transparent pixels its own blur, so a layer's outline is not sharpened. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. FX-SHARPEN-001 to 018 pin it. D-317 adds `threshold`, 0..255 (0), keyable, After Effects' Unsharp Mask Threshold: a channel with `|e_c - eb_c| < threshold / 255` keeps its value. At 0 nothing is kept, so the picture is as before; it is written to the file only when moved, keyed or already there. B-198 pins it. FX-SHARPEN-019 to 030 pin it too (D-363).

Diffusion (D-148, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `radius` in 0..500, a distance, `amount` in 0..100, each keyable and held inside its range at each frame, and `blend`, exactly `screen`, `lighten` or `normal`. `tools/diffusion_reference.py` is the reference. With `amount = 0` or `radius = 0` the output is the input. Otherwise `B` is the input through the Gaussian blur above at `sigma_px = radius / 3`, read at the input's own pixels, its bounds expansion cut off. For a pixel with `a > 0`, with `b = p.rgb / a` and `g = B.rgb / B.a` when `B.a > 0`, else `b`: `f = 1 - (1 - b)(1 - g)` (screen), `max(b, g)` per channel (lighten) or `g` (normal), and the output is `((b + amount / 100 * (f - b)) * a, a)`, not clamped. A pixel with `a = 0` stays as it is. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. For a draft, `radius` is scaled as a distance. On a drawing it runs on its own pixels before its transform (ADR-017). FX-DIFFUSE-001 to 018 pin it. D-364 (2026-10-08) adds a second pass: `second_amount` in 0..100, keyable and held inside its range at each frame, 0 when the file leaves it out, and `second_blend`, exactly `soft_light` or `overlay`, `soft_light` when the file leaves it out. With radius 0, or amount 0 and second amount 0, the output is the input. Otherwise, with `r = b + amount / 100 * (f - b)` the first pass's straight colour and `m` the layer modes' W3C overlay or soft light (D-301, `grade::mixer`), backdrop first: `s = srgb_to_linear(m(linear_to_srgb(clamp(r, 0, 1)), linear_to_srgb(clamp(g, 0, 1))))`, and the output is `((r + second_amount / 100 * (s - r)) * a, a)`, not clamped. Both passes share the one blur `B`. A file without the two settings is the rule above exactly, and is saved without them. `tools/diffusion_second_reference.py` is the reference; FX-DIFFUSE-019 to 032 pin it.

Wave warp (D-149, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `shape`, exactly `sine` or `triangle`; `height` in 0..1000 and `width` in 1..10000, both distances; `direction` in degrees, -3600..3600; `speed` in -360..360 degrees a frame; `phase` in -100000..100000 degrees, the numbers held inside their ranges at each frame; and `edges`, exactly `transparent` or `repeat`. `tools/wave_warp_reference.py` is the reference. With height 0 the output is the input and nothing grows. Otherwise, with `transparent`, the input grows by `g = ceil(height)` transparent pixels on every side; with `repeat`, `g = 0`. `t = u(direction) = (sin direction, -cos direction)`, exactly `(0, -1)`, `(1, 0)`, `(0, 1)` or `(-1, 0)` at whole multiples of 90, and `n = (-t.y, t.x)`. For an output pixel with centre `P`, at `(X, Y) = (x - o_x + 0.5, y - o_y + 0.5)` in drawing coordinates (`o` the growth; a grown pixel has negative coordinates), with `f` the composition frame, `s = t.x X + t.y Y`, `φ = radians(phase + speed * f)` and `w = sin(2π s / width + φ)` for `sine`, or `w = (2 / π) asin(sin(2π s / width + φ))` for `triangle`; the output is `bilinear(input, P - height w n)`, transparent outside the input, with `transparent`, or D-109's held sample, the point first held inside the input's pixel centres, with `repeat`. The bounds expansion is `g`. For a draft, `height` and `width` are scaled as distances. FX-WAVE-001 to 024 pin it.

Ripple (D-150, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `center`, two numbers, per cent of the drawing, each -1000..1000; `amplitude` in 0..1000, a distance; `wavelength` in 1..10000, a distance; `speed` in -360..360 degrees a frame; `phase` in -100000..100000 degrees; and `fade` in 0..100000, a distance; all held inside their ranges at each frame. `tools/ripple_reference.py` is the reference. At amplitude 0 the output is the input. Otherwise, at each pixel, with `P` its centre and `c = o + (center.x / 100 * w, center.y / 100 * h)` the centre point, both in the buffer (`o` the growth of effects before it), and `d = |P - c|`: at `d == 0` the output is the input pixel; else `w = sin(2 * pi * d / wavelength - (phase + speed * frame) * pi / 180)`, `f = 1` when fade is 0, else `f = max(0, 1 - d / fade)`, and the output is the bilinear sample of the input, transparent outside it, at `P + amplitude * w * f * (P - c) / d`, all four premultiplied channels alike. `frame` is the composition frame, filled in by the build as Noise's is, never saved. A pixel whose `w * f` is 0 is the input pixel exactly. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. A draft scales `amplitude`, `wavelength` and `fade`. FX-RIPPLE-001 to 026 pin it.

Twirl (D-151, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `angle` in -3600..3600 degrees, `radius` in 0..10000, a distance, and `center`, two numbers each in -1000..1000, all held inside their ranges at each frame. `tools/twirl_reference.py` is the reference. With angle 0 or radius 0 the output is the input. Otherwise, with `w, h` the drawing's own size, `o` the growth of effects before it, the centre `c = o + (center_x / 100 * w, center_y / 100 * h)`, `P` the centre of an output pixel and `d = |P - c|`: at `d >= radius` the output is the input pixel exactly; else `t = 1 - d / radius`, `β = radians(angle) * t^2`, and the output is `bilinear(input, c + R(-β)(P - c))`, transparent outside the input, with `R(β)(x, y) = (x cos β - y sin β, x sin β + y cos β)`, clockwise on the screen for positive β, so a positive angle turns the picture clockwise, most at the centre. Premultiplied channels are sampled alike. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. For a draft, `radius` is scaled as a distance. FX-TWIRL-001 to 021 pin it.

Bulge (D-152, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `center`, a point per cent of the drawing, each -1000..1000; `radius` in 0..10000, a distance; and `height` in -4..4; all held inside their ranges at each frame. `tools/bulge_reference.py` is the reference. With `height = 0` or `radius = 0` the output is the input. Otherwise, with `c = o + (center_x / 100 * w, center_y / 100 * h)` (`o` the growth of effects before it, `w` by `h` the drawing's own size), at each pixel with `P` its centre and `d = |P - c|`: at `d >= radius` the input pixel is kept exactly; else `r = d / radius`, `m = max(0, 1 - height * (1 - r)^2 / 2)`, and the output is the bilinear sample of the input at `c + m * (P - c)`, transparent outside the input. `m = 1` at `d = radius`, so the warp is continuous there and the rule has no cliff; at `d = 0` the sample is at `P` itself. A positive height swells the middle, a negative one pinches it. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion. A draft scales `radius`. FX-BULGE-001 to 023 pin it. D-310 adds `vertical_radius` and `taper_radius`, each in 0..10000, a distance, 0 when the file leaves it out, keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; a draft scales both. With `ry = vertical_radius` if above 0, else `radius`: when `ry = radius` the rule above holds and `t = 1 - d / radius`; otherwise `n = |((P - c)_x / radius, (P - c)_y / ry)|`, the input pixel is kept exactly at `n >= 1`, and `t = 1 - n`. With `taper_radius = T > 0` and `t < 1`, `e = d t / (1 - t)`, the distance from `P` to the edge along the ray from `c`, `s = min(1, e / T)` and the height used is `height * s^2 (3 - 2 s)`; else the height itself. Then `m = max(0, 1 - height_used * t^2 / 2)` and the output is as above. At both 0 the output is unchanged, so FX-BULGE-001 to 023 are unchanged. B-193 pins it.

Mirror (D-153, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `center`, two numbers, per cent of the drawing, each in -1000..1000, and `angle` in -3600..3600 degrees, both held inside their ranges at each frame. `tools/mirror_reference.py` is the reference. With `o` the growth of the effects before it and `w, h` the drawing's own size, `c = o + (center.x / 100 * w, center.y / 100 * h)`; `n = u(angle + 90)`, `u(t) = (sin t, -cos t)`, exactly `(0, -1)`, `(1, 0)`, `(0, 1)` or `(-1, 0)` when `t` is a whole multiple of 90, so angle 0 gives `n = (1, 0)`. For an output pixel with centre `P`, `δ = (P - c) · n`: at `δ >= 0` the output is the input pixel exactly; else it is `bilinear(input, P - 2 δ n)`, transparent outside the input. At `δ = 0` the two agree, so the choice is not a cliff. The output has the input's size: no bounds expansion, and nothing is scaled for a draft. FX-MIRROR-001 to 024 pin it.

Motion tile (D-154, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `output_width` and `output_height`, each in 100..1000 per cent, held inside the range at each frame, and `mirror`, exactly `off` or `on`. `tools/motion_tile_reference.py` is the reference. `w, h` is the input's size as it reaches the effect, the pixels an earlier effect grew included (Offset's). `gx = ceil(w (output_width / 100 - 1) / 2)` and `gy = ceil(h (output_height / 100 - 1) / 2)`; the output is `w + 2 gx` by `h + 2 gy`, its origin moved by `(gx, gy)`. For output pixel `(x, y)`: `i = x - gx`, `k = floor(i / w)`, `j = i - k w`, and with `on` and `k` odd, `j = w - 1 - j`; the row likewise from `y - gy`, `h` and `gy`; the output is the input pixel `(j, j_row)` exactly, all four channels. Both 100: the output is the input. The bounds expansion is `(gx, gy)`. For a draft nothing is scaled. FX-TILE-001 to 023 pin it. D-304 adds `tile_center`, two numbers each in -1000..1000 per cent, `[50, 50]` when the file leaves it out, and `tile_width` and `tile_height`, each in 1..1000 per cent, 100 when left out, all keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. At `[50, 50]`, 100 and 100 the rule above holds exactly. Otherwise, with `s = tile_width / 100`, `t = w s`, `c = tile_center_x / 100 * w`, the place `p` along a row (drawing coordinates, a grown pixel's negative) reads the input at `r(p)`: `u = p - (c - t / 2)`, `k = floor(u / t)`, `f = u - k t`, with `on` and `k` odd `f = t - f`, and `r(p) = clamp(f / s, 0.5, w - 0.5)`; a column likewise with `tile_height`, `h` and `tile_center_y`. With `m_x = clamp(ceil(1 / s), 1, 16)` and `m_y` likewise, output pixel `(x, y)` is the mean over `a < m_x`, `b < m_y` of `bilinear(input, r(x - gx + (a + 0.5) / m_x), r_row(y - gy + (b + 0.5) / m_y))`, all four channels. The output size and bounds expansion are as above.

Linear Wipe (D-155, accepted on 2026-09-26): parameters `completion` in 0..100, `angle` in degrees in -3600..3600 and `feather` in 0..10000, a distance scaled for a draft; all keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. `tools/linear_wipe_reference.py` is the reference. At completion 0 the output is the input exactly; at 100 every pixel is `(0, 0, 0, 0)`, the size unchanged.
