The layer never grows; a draft divides the radius by its scale. A Light Rays whose settings hold `length` or `threshold` is D-124's form and draws by D-124's rule. Drawn on the card by warp mode 33, the card's zoom and spin blurs and Light Rays' rays pass with a mode flag. FX-RAYSCC-001 to 039 pin it.

Glue Gun (D-424, 2026-10-10), CycoreFX's CC Glue Gun, whose manual names the controls but gives no formula, ranges or defaults, so this rule is ours, nothing ported. `tools/glue_gun_reference.py` is the reference. `core.glue_gun`: `brush_position`, per cent, -1000..1000 ((50, 50)); `stroke_width`, pixels, 0..500 (20); `density`, blobs a frame, 0..100 (5); `time_span`, seconds, 0..100 (1); `reflection`, 0..100 (50); `strength`, 0..100 (50); `paint_style`, "plain" or "wobbly" (plain); `wobble_width` and `wobble_height`, pixels, 0..1000 (10); `wobble_speed`, turns a second, 0..100 (1); and Blobbylize's `light_intensity`, `light_color`, `light_type`, `light_height`, `light_position`, `light_direction`, `ambient`, `diffuse`, `specular`, `roughness`, `metal`, with its ranges and defaults. The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame; only `brush_position` is read at frames before, every other setting as it is now. At composition frame f, fps frames a second, in linear light, premultiplied, O the layer: **the history** n = f - the layer's in point with time_span 0, else floor(time_span fps + 1/2); P_j, j = 0..n, the brush at frame f - j in pixels. **The blobs** k = ceil((f - n) d) .. floor(f d), d = density; u = (f d - k) / d, j = floor(u), t = u - j; blob k at P_j + (P_j+1 - P_j) t; wobbly moves it by (wobble_width sin(2 pi (s T + frac(0.6180339887498949 k))), wobble_height sin(2 pi (s T + frac(0.7548776662466927 k)))), T = f / fps, s = wobble_speed. **The field** r = stroke_width / 2, R = r (1 + strength / 100), T0 = (1 - (r / R)^2)^2; q = |p - c|^2 / R^2; F = sum over q < 1 of (1 - q)^2, G = sum of -4 (1 - q) (p - c) / R^2; s = (F - T0) / |G|; covering a = clamp(s + 1/2, 0, 1) (with |G| = 0, a = 1 if F > T0, else 0). **The tube** m = clamp(1 - s / r, 0, 1), N = (-m Gx / |G|, -m Gy / |G|, sqrt(1 - m^2)), (0, 0, 1) with |G| = 0. **The colour** C = O at p - (reflection / 100) 2 r (Nx, Ny), the bilinear sample (transparent outside), straight, black where clear. **The light** Blobbylize's (D-379, steps 6 and 7) on C and N. **Laid on** lit a + O (1 - a), covering a + O.a (1 - a). The layer never grows; a draft scales stroke_width, the wobble's width and height and a point light's height. Drawn on the card by warp mode 34 from the blobs the processor lays. FX-GLUE-001 to 027 pin it.

Threads (D-425, 2026-10-10), CycoreFX's CC Threads, whose manual names the controls but gives no formula, ranges or defaults, so this rule is ours, nothing ported. `tools/threads_reference.py` is the reference. `core.threads`: `width` and `height`, pixels, 1..1000 (50); `overlaps`, 1..10 (1), taken whole; `direction`, degrees, -3600..3600 (0); `center`, per cent, -1000..1000 ((50, 50)); `coverage`, `shadowing`, `texture`, 0..100 (90, 50, 0). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. With O the layer (linear, premultiplied), W by H, p a pixel's centre: c = (center_x W, center_y H) / 100; d = p - c; t = direction in radians; u = dx cos t + dy sin t, v = -dx sin t + dy cos t. i = floor(u / width), e = u - (i + 1/2) width; j = floor(v / height), f = v - (j + 1/2) height; hw = coverage / 100 width / 2, hh = coverage / 100 height / 2. A thread at offset x, half width h: share m = max(0, min(x + 1/2, h) - max(x - 1/2, -h)); shade 1 - T min(1, (x / h)^2), T = texture / 100 (1 with h = 0). n = floor(overlaps); the warp (e, hw) is over the weft (f, hh) where (i + j) mod 2n < n, else the weft over. The thread beneath darkened by 1 - S (1 + g) / 2, S = shadowing / 100, g = clamp(1 - (|x_up| - h_up) / h_up, 0, 1) (0 with h_up = 0). Colour O (m_up shade_up + m_down shade_down dark (1 - m_up)), alpha O.a (m_up + m_down (1 - m_up)). The layer never grows; a draft scales width and height. Drawn on the card by warp mode 35. FX-THREADS-001 to 028 pin it.

Lens Flare (D-426, 2026-10-10), After Effects' Lens Flare built from the parts of Video Copilot's Optical Flares. No formula was available, so this rule is ours, nothing ported. `tools/lens_flare_reference.py` is the reference and holds each lens's parts (`layer_fx::FLARE_LENSES` holds the same). `core.lens_flare`: `flare_center`, per cent, -1000..1000 ((30, 30)); `flare_brightness`, 0..300 (100); `lens_type`, "zoom", "35mm" or "105mm" ("zoom"); `blend_with_original`, 0..100 (0). The numbers are keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. With O the layer (linear, premultiplied), W by H, p a pixel's centre: F = flare_center (W, H) / 100, M = (W, H) / 2, D = hypot(W, H). Each part (kind, t, r, w, n, colour) sits at P = F + t (M - F), R = r D; d = |p - P|. A glow gives exp(-(d / R)^2); a ring exp(-((d - R) / (w D))^2); a disc clamp((R - d) / (w D) + 1/2, 0, 1); a star exp(-d / R) ((1 + cos(n a)) / 2)^w, a the angle of p - P (0 at d = 0). L = flare_brightness / 100 times the sum of colour times part. The flared pixel is (O.rgb + L, O.a + (1 - O.a) min(1, max L)); the result O + (1 - blend_with_original / 100) (flared - O). The layer never grows; a draft changes nothing (the sizes are shares of D). Drawn on the card by warp mode 36, the parts placed on the processor. FX-FLARE-001 to 019 pin it.

Arbitrary Map (D-395, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' PS Arbitrary Map. `tools/arbitrary_map_reference.py` is the reference. `core.arbitrary_map`: `map`, the id of a `lut` asset of the project or "" for none ("" when added, which leaves the layer as it is); `phase`, -255 to 255 levels, keyable, 0 when added; `apply_to_alpha`, "off" or "on", "off" when added. The file is read by Adobe's Photoshop File Formats Specification: no header, one or more tables of 256 bytes, entry x the value x becomes. One table is the master for every colour channel; exactly three are red, green and blue; any other count is the master then the channels in turn (four: master, red, green, blue; five: those and alpha). A channel without a table is straight. Empty, a length not a whole number of tables, or more than five tables is refused with the reason in words. `phase` cycles each held table right: T'(u) = T(v), mixed linearly between entries floor(v) and floor(v) + 1 (past 255 is entry 0), at v = (u - phase) mod 256. The colour tables become one 256-entry table per colour, F_c[x] = M'(C_c'(x)), the channel's then the master's as Curves (D-111), applied by Color Lookup's 1D rule (D-182) to the encoded straight colour, u = 255 e. With `apply_to_alpha` "on" and an alpha table, the covering then goes through it as Curves' alpha curve (D-302): the straight colour kept, a pixel that did not show and now does black. A `map` naming no `lut` asset, a missing file and an unreadable file leave the layer as it is with EFFECT_PARAMETER_INVALID, MEDIA_MISSING and MEDIA_DECODE_FAILED. Nothing grows; a draft changes nothing. **Deviations:** Adobe does not publish Phase's range or unit, so -255 to 255 levels is ours; a file of more than five tables (CMYK or multichannel) is refused rather than guessed. Drawn on the card in the tone pass (mode 11, Color Lookup's), except with Apply Phase Map To Alpha on over a file with an alpha table, which stays on the processor as Curves' alpha curve does. FX-AMAP-001 to 022 pin it.

Selective Color (D-396, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' Selective Color (Photoshop's adjustment). `tools/selective_color_reference.py` is the reference. `core.selective_color`: `method`, "relative" (when added, as Photoshop starts) or "absolute"; `reds`, `yellows`, `greens`, `cyans`, `blues`, `magentas`, `whites`, `neutrals`, `blacks`, each four per cents, cyan, magenta, yellow and black, -100 to 100, keyable, all 0 when added; all written. The rule is Clement Boesch's reverse-engineering of Photoshop's, published with FFmpeg's selectivecolor filter, worked in fractions. At a pixel with covering a > 0, e = linear_to_srgb(clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1)) is its encoded straight colour, with largest, middle and smallest mx, md, mn. Each family's weight w: reds, greens and blues mx - md where red, green or blue is the largest (ties count for each); cyans, magentas and yellows md - mn where red, green or blue is the smallest; whites 2 mn - 1; neutrals 1 - (|mx - 0.5| + |mn - 0.5|); blacks 1 - 2 mx. For each family with w > 0, with s its cyan, magenta or yellow over 100 for channel red, green or blue and k its black over 100, the channel moves by w times t, t = (-1 - s) k - s, times 1 - e_c when relative, held inside -e_c to 1 - e_c; the moves add, and the result is held inside 0 to 1, back to linear and times a. Every weight is continuous in the colour. Every amount 0 is the input. Nothing grows; a draft changes nothing. **Deviations:** FFmpeg works on whole 8-bit levels and rounds each family's move to a level, and counts a pixel as white only when its smallest channel is above 128, where in fractions a smallest channel of exactly 128 is a white of weight 1/255; this program works in fractions. FFmpeg starts absolute; Photoshop and this program start relative. After Effects' Colors menu only chooses which family's sliders are shown, so it is not a setting. Drawn on the card in the tone pass (mode 60), one pixel, so it joins fused runs. FX-SELC-001 to 023 pin it.

Shadow/Highlight (D-400, 2026-10-09), after After Effects' Shadow/Highlight. `tools/shadow_highlight_reference.py` is the reference. `core.shadow_highlight`: `shadow_amount` and `highlight_amount` 0 to 100 (50 and 0 when added), `shadow_tonal_width` and `highlight_tonal_width` 0 to 100 (50), `shadow_radius` and `highlight_radius` 0 to 500 pixels (30), `color_correction` 0 to 100 (20), each keyable, all written. The rule is darktable's shadows and highlights (src/iop/shadhi.c, Gaussian softening), not ported. At a pixel with covering a > 0, b = clamp(p.rgb / a, 0, 1) its straight linear colour; its CIE L*a*b* on the sRGB primaries' own white (X, Y, Z = M b, M the sRGB matrix of IEC 61966-2-1, the white M (1, 1, 1)), scaled l = L* / 100, p = a* / 128, q = b* / 128; a pixel with a = 0 has l = 0 for the blur and is left as it is. l over the layer is blurred by Gaussian Blur's kernel (sigma the radius, the edge pixels repeated): ls at the shadow radius, lh at the highlight radius, a radius 0 the pixel's own; ts = 1 - ls, th = 1 - lh. s = 2 shadow / 100, h = 2 highlight / 100, cs = min(1 - shadow width / 100, 0.99), ch likewise, k = colour correction / 100. Highlights first: n = h^2; while n > 0, with x = clamp(1 - th / (1 - ch), 0, 1), o = min(n, 1) x, lb = (th - 1/2) sign(1 - l) + 1/2, l' = l (1 - o) + (l > 1/2 ? 1 - (1 - 2 (l - 1/2)) (1 - lb) : 2 l lb) o, f = l' r(l) (1 - k) + (1 - l') r(1 - l) k, p and q times (1 - o + f o), l = l', n less 1. Then the shadows, n = s^2, x = clamp(ts / (1 - cs) - cs / (1 - cs), 0, 1), lb from ts, f = l' r(l) k + (1 - l') r(1 - l) (1 - k). r(v) is 1 / v, held at plus or minus 10^6 where |v| <= 10^-6; sign(0) is 1. Back to linear by the inverse, each channel held inside 0 to 1, times a; the covering never changes. Both amounts 0 is the input; black and white stay as they are. A draft scales both radii with the picture. **Deviations:** two radii and two tonal widths where darktable has one radius and one compress; darktable's white point adjustment left at 0; L*a*b* on sRGB's white, not D50; the result held inside 0 to 1; Gaussian, not bilateral. After Effects' Auto Amounts, Temporal Smoothing, Scene Detect, Midtone Contrast and Black and White Clip are not settings (D-400). Drawn on the card as a lightness pass, the held Gaussian at each radius (the second copied into green when the radii differ) and the rule in double precision (the cube root from single precision with two Newton steps). FX-SHHI-001 to 021 pin it.

Radial Shadow (D-211, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/radial_shadow_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `color`, `#rrggbb`; `opacity` in 0..100; `light`, a point in per cent of the drawing's own width and height, each in -1000..1000; `distance` in 0..1000; `softness` in 0..500 pixels; `render`, `regular` or `glass_edge`; `color_influence` in 0..100; `shadow_only`, `off` or `on`; all but `render` and `shadow_only` keyable and held inside their ranges at each frame. With `L` the light in the drawing's own space as Bulge's centre (D-152) and `k = 1 + distance / 100`, the layer, its buffer spanning `left..right` across and `top..bottom` down, first grows across by `gx = min(ceil(distance / 100 * max(Lx - left, right - Lx)), its width)` and down by `gy`, the same with the height; each pixel whose centre is `P` there takes document 21's bilinear sample of the input, all four channels, at `L + (P - L) / k`, transparent outside it; that is blurred by document 21's Gaussian at sigma `softness / 3`, transparent edges, which grows it by the blur's reach. With `S` a pixel of that and `o = opacity / 100`, the shadow is `(C * S.a * o, S.a * o)` for `regular`, `C` the colour's linear value, or `(((1 - f) * C * S.a + f * S.rgb) * o, S.a * o)` for `glass_edge`, `f = color_influence / 100`; the output is the input `I` over it, `I + shadow * (1 - I.a)`, or with `shadow_only` `on` the shadow alone. A draft scales `softness`. FX-RSHADOW-001 to 025 pin it.

Extract (D-212, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/extract_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `channel`, `luminance`, `red`, `green`, `blue` or `alpha`; `black_point`, `white_point`, `black_softness` and `white_softness`, each in 0..255, keyable and held inside that range at each frame; `invert`, `off` or `on`. At a pixel with covering `a > 0`, with `e` its encoded straight colour as Threshold (D-138) takes it, the value `v` is `255 Y(e)` with Threshold's luma for `luminance`, 255 times that channel of `e` for `red`, `green` or `blue`, and `255 a` for `alpha`. With `b`, `w`, `s` and `t` the four numbers, `low = clamp((v - b) / s, 0, 1)` when `s > 0`, else 1 when `v + 1e-4 >= b` and 0 otherwise; `high = clamp((w - v) / t, 0, 1)` when `t > 0`, else 1 when `v - 1e-4 <= w` and 0 otherwise; `m = low * high`, or `1 - low * high` with `invert` `on`; the output is the pixel with all four channels times `m`. A pixel with `a = 0` is left as it is. The layer does not grow and a draft changes nothing. FX-EXTRACT-001 to 027 pin it.

Bevel Alpha and Bevel Edges (D-213, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/bevel_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `edge_thickness`, `0..200` pixels for `core.bevel_alpha` and `0..0.5` for `core.bevel_edges`; `light_angle`, `-3600..3600`; `light_intensity`, `0..1`; each keyable and held inside its range at each frame; `light_color`, `#rrggbb`. With `u = (sin θ, -cos θ)` for `θ = light_angle`, exact at whole quarter turns, each pixel has a slope `s` in `-1..1`. Bevel Alpha: `H` is the covering blurred by this document's Gaussian at sigma `T / 2`, `T` the thickness, transparent outside the layer and 0 beyond the blur's reach; `gx = (H(x+1, y) - H(x-1, y)) / 2`, `gy = (H(x, y+1) - H(x, y-1)) / 2`, and `s = clamp(-1.25 T (gx ux + gy uy), -1, 1)`. Bevel Edges: `T` is the thickness times the smaller of the layer's width and height as it reaches the effect; a pixel whose centre is nearer than `T` to the nearest side of the layer's rectangle, the first of left, top, right and bottom among equals, has `s = n . u` with `n` that side's outward normal, and every other pixel `s = 0`. At a pixel with covering `a > 0`, `L` the colour's linear value and `I` the intensity: `p + (L a - p) I s` when `s > 0`, `p (1 - I |s|)` when `s < 0`, the covering unchanged. A pixel with `a = 0` is left as it is. The layer does not grow. A draft scales Bevel Alpha's `T`. FX-BEVEL-001 to 026 pin it.

Block Dissolve (D-214, accepted on 2026-09-28). `tools/block_dissolve_reference.py` is the reference. Parameters: `completion`, `0..100`; `block_width` and `block_height`, `1..10000` pixels; `feather`, `0..10000` pixels; each keyable and held inside its range at each frame. At completion 0 the layer is unchanged; at 100 every channel of every pixel is 0. Otherwise `L = completion / 100`. With `(X, Y)` a pixel centre in the drawing's own coordinates, from its top left corner as the layer reaches the effect, the block `(i, j)` is `[i bw, (i+1) bw) x [j bh, (j+1) bh)` for every whole `i` and `j`, past the drawing's edges too, with the number `r = (u + 1) / 2`, `u` D-119's hash for seed 0 and the whole numbers `(i, j, 0, 0)`, and `K = 1` when `r >= L`, else 0. With feather `f = 0`, `k = K` of the block holding `(X, Y)`; with `f > 0`, `k` is the mean of `K` over the square `[X - f/2, X + f/2] x [Y - f/2, Y + f/2]`, held in `0..1`. The output is `p k` on all four premultiplied channels. The layer does not grow. A draft scales `bw` and `bh`, never below 1, and `f`. FX-BDISSOLVE-001 to 019 pin it.

## Evaluation bounds and ROI

Every effect declares input bounds expansion. Transform/mask/matte operations declare the region they can affect. Cache keys include all parameters that alter pixels or bounds. An optimization may skip pixels outside ROI only if output equals full-frame reference math within test tolerance.

An export may draw several frames at the same time. Each frame is drawn from the project alone, as it always was, so drawing frames together changes no pixel. Files are written, and notes added to the report, in frame order. D-231, B-160.

## Numeric tolerance

CPU reference scalar tests should use exact/near-exact float comparisons appropriate to the operation. GPU comparison tolerance begins at absolute channel error <= 1e-5 for simple arithmetic fixtures and requires per-effect declared tolerances for filters. A tolerance increase requires evidence, not convenience.

## Display pipeline

Viewer checkerboard, alpha-only display, overlays, selection outlines and draft-resolution indicators are presentation layers. They must not alter cached final pixels or export results.

D-311: a composition's `background_color` is presentation too. The frame, a PNG, an EXR, an animated PNG, a GIF and a composition layer drawing it are unchanged. The viewer shows it behind the frame with the checkerboard off. An MP4 has no alpha: each 8-bit straight pixel `(e, a)` of the frame becomes `round(e a + 255 linear_to_srgb(bg) (1 - a))` per channel with alpha 255, in encoded values, the same space the MP4 was laid over black in before, so a black background gives the MP4 as before. B-194 pins it.

## Deferred rendering questions

Intersecting transparent planes, motion blur (its own contract above, ADR-019, accepted on 2026-09-28), frame blending (its own contract above, ADR-020, accepted on 2026-09-29), depth of field, HDR display transforms and higher-order resampling are separate contracts. They must not be implied by this G1 specification. G2 camera projection was one of them until D-58, which the owner accepted on 2026-09-15 and B-13c built; it is specified under Camera and depth above, and it deliberately specifies none of the rest - in particular a camera that tilts or turns, which would need a perspective transform rather than the affine one written there.

Related documents: 08, 18, 20, 25 and 27.

---

## Tile contract (added in version 0.3, ADR-011)

The unit of render work is a tile: a fixed-size rectangular region of the output frame, composited independently of every other tile in that frame. A frame evaluation divides the output extent into tiles and distributes them across worker threads. Tiles within one frame evaluation never depend on one another.

All math specified above is defined per pixel and is therefore unchanged by tiling. Tiling changes only the order and grouping of evaluation, and must not change results: a tiled render and a hypothetical whole-frame render of the same request must be byte-identical, and B-05a proves this rather than assuming it.

Tile size is a tunable measured on the reference machine, not a constant chosen in advance. Too small wastes scheduling overhead; too large starves threads at the frame edges.

### Spatial support and margins

An operation whose output pixel depends only on the corresponding input pixel is tile-safe without qualification. This covers transforms sampled per output pixel, blending, opacity and color conversion, which is the whole of G1-core.

An operation whose output pixel depends on a neighborhood, such as blur, requires the tile to be evaluated with a margin of the operation's radius, then cropped. The margin is part of the operation's contract and must be declared by the operation rather than assumed by the renderer. An operation with unbounded support cannot be tiled without an explicit strategy and must not be added casually.

This is a direct reason R-05 sits in G1-rest: the blur in that requirement is the first operation that will exercise margin handling, and it deserves its own fixtures rather than arriving inside a milestone that has none.

### Relationship to a future GPU path

The tile decomposition is also the decomposition a GPU compute dispatch needs. Committing to it now is what makes ADR-006, which defers GPU work entirely, a deferral rather than a dead end: adding a GPU path later becomes a port of tile execution, not a rewrite of the renderer.

This is the one piece of forward design in the pack that is not justified by present need, and it is accepted deliberately, because retrofitting tiling onto a whole-frame renderer would be substantially more expensive than adopting it from the start.

### Determinism

Tile results must not depend on the number of worker threads, the order of completion, or scheduling. Two renders of the same request on the same build produce identical bytes, which SP-04 establishes and B-10 re-verifies against the exported sequence.
